<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vigiles_du_roi_Charles_VII_10.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Joan in red dress being bound to a stake as a group of men look on" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Vigiles_du_roi_Charles_VII_10.jpg/290px-Vigiles_du_roi_Charles_VII_10.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="188" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Vigiles_du_roi_Charles_VII_10.jpg/435px-Vigiles_du_roi_Charles_VII_10.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Vigiles_du_roi_Charles_VII_10.jpg/580px-Vigiles_du_roi_Charles_VII_10.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1642" data-file-height="1066" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vigiles_du_roi_Charles_VII_10.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Miniature of Joan's Execution from <i>The Vigils of King Charles VII</i>, anonymous (<abbr title="circa">c.</abbr> 1484, <a href="/wiki/Biblioth%C3%A8que_nationale_de_France" title="Bibliothèque nationale de France">Bibliothèque nationale de France</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>On 28 May, Cauchon went to Joan's cell, along with several other clerics. According to the trial record, Joan said that she had gone back to wearing men's clothes because it was more fitting that she dress like a man while being held with male guards, and that the judges had broken their promise to let her go to mass and to release her from her chains. She stated that if they fulfilled their promises and placed her in a decent prison, she would be obedient.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBullough1974&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable2777140seq9_1389&#93;Crane1996&#91;httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_302&#93;Hobbins2005&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailstrialofjoanofarc00danipage24_24&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage132_132–133&#93;Sullivan1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage132_132–133&#93;_257-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBullough1974[httpswwwjstororgstable2777140seq9_1389]Crane1996[httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_302]Hobbins2005[httpsarchiveorgdetailstrialofjoanofarc00danipage24_24]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage132_132–133]Sullivan1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage132_132–133]-257">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> When Cauchon asked about her visions, Joan stated that the voices had blamed her for abjuring out of fear, and that she would not deny them again. As Joan's abjuration had required her to deny her visions, this was sufficient to convict her of relapsing into heresy and to condemn her to death.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage217_217&#93;Hobbins2005&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailstrialofjoanofarc00danipage24_24–25&#93;_258-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage217_217]Hobbins2005[httpsarchiveorgdetailstrialofjoanofarc00danipage24_24–25]-258">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> The next day, forty-two assessors were summoned to decide Joan's fate. Two recommended that she be abandoned to the secular courts immediately; the rest recommended that the abjuration be read to her again and explained.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage218_218–219&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage134_134–135&#93;_259-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage218_218–219]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage134_134–135]-259">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup> In the end, they voted unanimously that Joan was a relapsed heretic, and should be abandoned to the secular power, the English, for punishment.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHobbins2005&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailstrialofjoanofarc00danipage198_198&#93;Sullivan1999Taylor2006&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage139_139&#93;_260-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHobbins2005[httpsarchiveorgdetailstrialofjoanofarc00danipage198_198]Sullivan1999Taylor2006[httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage139_139]-260">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At about the age of nineteen, Joan was executed on 30 May 1431. In the morning, she was allowed to receive the <a href="/wiki/Sacraments_of_the_Catholic_Church" title="Sacraments of the Catholic Church">sacraments</a> despite court process requiring they be denied to heretics.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage219_219–220&#93;Harrison2014&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage296_296&#93;Lucie-Smith1976&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage279_279–280&#93;_261-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage219_219–220]Harrison2014[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage296_296]Lucie-Smith1976[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage279_279–280]-261">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup> She was then taken to Rouen's Vieux-Marché (Old Marketplace), where she was publicly read her sentence of condemnation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESullivan1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage148_148&#93;Taylor2006&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage225_225&#93;_262-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESullivan1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage148_148]Taylor2006[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage225_225]-262">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup> At this point, she should have been turned over to the appropriate authority, the bailiff of Rouen, for secular sentencing, but instead was delivered directly to the English<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage223_223&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage135_135&#93;_263-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage223_223]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage135_135]-263">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup> and tied to a tall plastered pillar for <a href="/wiki/Death_by_burning" title="Death by burning">execution by burning</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELucie-Smith1976&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage282_281–282&#93;Michelet1855&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcmaidofo00michpage228_228–229&#93;_264-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELucie-Smith1976[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage282_281–282]Michelet1855[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcmaidofo00michpage228_228–229]-264">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup> She asked to view a cross as she died, and was given one an English soldier made from a stick, which she kissed and placed next to her chest.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage222_223&#93;Lowell1896&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen359_341&#93;Michelet1855&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcmaidofo00michpage238_238&#93;_265-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage222_223]Lowell1896[httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen359_341]Michelet1855[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcmaidofo00michpage238_238]-265">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup> A processional <a href="/wiki/Crucifix" title="Crucifix">crucifix</a> was fetched from the church of Saint-Saveur. She embraced it before her hands were bound, and it was held before her eyes during her execution.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage223_223&#93;Lucie-Smith1976&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage282_282–283&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage136_136&#93;_266-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage223_223]Lucie-Smith1976[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage282_282–283]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage136_136]-266">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup> After her death, her remains were thrown into the <a href="/wiki/Seine" title="Seine">Seine</a> River.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage223_223&#93;Lowell1896&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen359_341&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage137_137&#93;_267-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage223_223]Lowell1896[httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen359_341]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage137_137]-267">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Aftermath_and_rehabilitation_trial">Aftermath and rehabilitation trial</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Rehabilitation_trial_of_Joan_of_Arc" title="Rehabilitation trial of Joan of Arc">Rehabilitation trial of Joan of Arc</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Monument_comm%C3%A9moratif_de_la_r%C3%A9habilitation_de_Jeanne_d%E2%80%99Arc.jpg" class="image"><img alt="A group of highly detailed and realistic painted plaster statues depicting four men wearing various ecclesiastical garments. They are arranged in a complex composition around a representation of Joan of Arc on a set of stairs." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Monument_comm%C3%A9moratif_de_la_r%C3%A9habilitation_de_Jeanne_d%E2%80%99Arc.jpg/220px-Monument_comm%C3%A9moratif_de_la_r%C3%A9habilitation_de_Jeanne_d%E2%80%99Arc.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="202" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Monument_comm%C3%A9moratif_de_la_r%C3%A9habilitation_de_Jeanne_d%E2%80%99Arc.jpg/330px-Monument_comm%C3%A9moratif_de_la_r%C3%A9habilitation_de_Jeanne_d%E2%80%99Arc.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Monument_comm%C3%A9moratif_de_la_r%C3%A9habilitation_de_Jeanne_d%E2%80%99Arc.jpg/440px-Monument_comm%C3%A9moratif_de_la_r%C3%A9habilitation_de_Jeanne_d%E2%80%99Arc.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3484" data-file-height="3198" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Monument_comm%C3%A9moratif_de_la_r%C3%A9habilitation_de_Jeanne_d%E2%80%99Arc.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Joan in the foreground, facing figures from her rehabilitation trial in the <i>Monument Commemorating the Rehabilitation of Joan of Arc</i>, a plaster work by <a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%89mile_Pinchon&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Émile Pinchon (page does not exist)">Émile Pinchon</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89mile_Pinchon" class="extiw" title="fr:Émile Pinchon">fr</a>&#93;</span> (1909, <a href="/wiki/Noyon_Cathedral" title="Noyon Cathedral">Cathédrale Notre-Dame de Noyon</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-269" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-269">&#91;f&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>The military situation was not changed by Joan's execution. Her triumphs had raised Armagnac morale, and the English were not able to regain momentum.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAllmand1988&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailshundredyearsware0000allmpage57_57&#93;CurryHoskinsRichardsonSpencer2015&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsagincourtcompani0000currpage106_106&#93;Fuller1954&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsmilitaryhistoryo01fullpage496_496–497&#93;_270-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllmand1988[httpsarchiveorgdetailshundredyearsware0000allmpage57_57]CurryHoskinsRichardsonSpencer2015[httpsarchiveorgdetailsagincourtcompani0000currpage106_106]Fuller1954[httpsarchiveorgdetailsmilitaryhistoryo01fullpage496_496–497]-270">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> Charles remained king of France,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAllmand1988&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailshundredyearsware0000allmpage57_57&#93;Fuller1954&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsmilitaryhistoryo01fullpage490_490&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage166_166&#93;_271-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllmand1988[httpsarchiveorgdetailshundredyearsware0000allmpage57_57]Fuller1954[httpsarchiveorgdetailsmilitaryhistoryo01fullpage490_490]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage166_166]-271">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup> despite a rival coronation held for the ten-year-old Henry VI of England at <a href="/wiki/Notre_Dame_de_Paris" class="mw-redirect" title="Notre Dame de Paris">Notre-Dame cathedral</a> in Paris in 1431.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBarker2009&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsconquestenglishk0000barkpage229_229&#93;_272-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarker2009[httpsarchiveorgdetailsconquestenglishk0000barkpage229_229]-272">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> In 1435, the Burgundians signed the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Arras_(1435)" class="mw-redirect" title="Treaty of Arras (1435)">Treaty of Arras</a>, abandoning their alliance with England.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBarker2009&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsconquestenglishk0000barkpage228_228&#93;DeVries1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsisbn_9780750918053page186_186&#93;Fuller1954&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsmilitaryhistoryo01fullpage494_494&#93;_273-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarker2009[httpsarchiveorgdetailsconquestenglishk0000barkpage228_228]DeVries1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsisbn_9780750918053page186_186]Fuller1954[httpsarchiveorgdetailsmilitaryhistoryo01fullpage494_494]-273">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup> Twenty-two years after Joan's death, the war ended with a French victory at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Castillon" title="Battle of Castillon">Battle of Castillon</a> in 1453,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAllmand1988&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailshundredyearsware0000allmpage36_36&#93;Burne1956&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsagincourtwarmili0000burnpage342_342&#93;_274-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllmand1988[httpsarchiveorgdetailshundredyearsware0000allmpage36_36]Burne1956[httpsarchiveorgdetailsagincourtwarmili0000burnpage342_342]-274">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup> and the English were expelled from all of France except <a href="/wiki/Calais" title="Calais">Calais</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECastor2015&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page230_230&#93;Gies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage231_231&#93;_275-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECastor2015[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page230_230]Gies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage231_231]-275">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Joan's execution created a political liability for Charles, implying that his consecration as the king of France had been achieved through the actions of a heretic.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECastor2015&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page224_224&#93;Gies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage230_230&#93;Harrison2014&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage313_313–314&#93;Vale1974&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailscharlesvii0000valepage62_62&#93;_276-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECastor2015[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page224_224]Gies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage230_230]Harrison2014[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage313_313–314]Vale1974[httpsarchiveorgdetailscharlesvii0000valepage62_62]-276">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup> On 15 February 1450, a few months after he regained Rouen, Charles ordered Guillaume Bouillé, a theologian and <a href="/wiki/List_of_rectors_of_the_University_of_Paris" title="List of rectors of the University of Paris">former rector of the University of Paris</a>, to open an inquest.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPernoud1955&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage3_3–4&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage189_189&#93;_277-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPernoud1955[httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage3_3–4]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage189_189]-277">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup> In a brief investigation, Bouillé interviewed seven witnesses of Joan's trial and concluded that the judgment of Joan as a heretic was arbitrary. She had been a prisoner of war treated as a political prisoner, and was put to death without basis.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage230_230&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage149_149–155&#93;_278-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage230_230]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage149_149–155]-278">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup>
Bouillé's report could not overturn the verdict but it opened the way for the later retrial.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELightbody1961&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage121_121&#93;Pernoud1955&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage18_318&#93;_279-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELightbody1961[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage121_121]Pernoud1955[httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage18_318]-279">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1452, a second inquest into Joan's trial was opened by <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">Cardinal</a> <a href="/wiki/Guillaume_d%27Estouteville" title="Guillaume d&#39;Estouteville">Guillaume d'Estouteville</a>, papal legate and relative of Charles, and <a href="/wiki/Jean_Br%C3%A9hal" title="Jean Bréhal">Jean Bréhal</a>, the recently-appointed Inquisitor of France,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECastor2015&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page228_228–229&#93;Lightbody1961&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage122_122&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage151_151&#93;_280-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECastor2015[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page228_228–229]Lightbody1961[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage122_122]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage151_151]-280">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup> who interviewed about 20 witnesses.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECastor2015&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page228_228–229&#93;Lucie-Smith1976&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage4_4&#93;_281-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECastor2015[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page228_228–229]Lucie-Smith1976[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage4_4]-281">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup> The inquest was guided by 27 articles describing how Joan's trial had been biased.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage152_152–155&#93;_282-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage152_152–155]-282">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup> Immediately after the inquest, d'Estouteville went to Orléans on 9 June and granted an <a href="/wiki/Indulgence" title="Indulgence">indulgence</a> to those who participated in the ceremonies in Joan's honor on 8 May commemorating the lifting of the siege.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPernoud1955&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage34_34&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190&#93;_283-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPernoud1955[httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage34_34]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190]-283">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For the next two years d'Estouteville and Bréhal worked on the case.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELightbody1961&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage122_122–123&#93;Lowell1896&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen368_350–351&#93;Murray1902&#91;httpswwwgutenbergorgfiles5738957389-h57389-hhtmPage_372_372&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190&#93;_284-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELightbody1961[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage122_122–123]Lowell1896[httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen368_350–351]Murray1902[httpswwwgutenbergorgfiles5738957389-h57389-hhtmPage_372_372]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190]-284">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup> Bréhal forwarded a petition from Joan's mother, Isabelle, and Joan's two brothers Jean and Pierre, to <a href="/wiki/Pope_Nicholas_V" title="Pope Nicholas V">Pope Nicholas V</a> in 1454.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPernoud1962&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcbyherse00pernpage264_264&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190&#93;_285-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPernoud1962[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcbyherse00pernpage264_264]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190]-285">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup> Bréhal submitted a summary of his findings to theologians and lawyers in France and Italy,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELightbody1961&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage122_128&#93;Lowell1896&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen367_350&#93;_286-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELightbody1961[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage122_128]Lowell1896[httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen367_350]-286">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup> as well as a professor at the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Vienna" title="University of Vienna">University of Vienna</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPernoud1955&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage37_37&#93;_287-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPernoud1955[httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage37_37]-287">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup> most of whom gave opinions favorable to Joan.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage235_235&#93;Lightbody1961&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage122_122&#93;_288-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage235_235]Lightbody1961[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage122_122]-288">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup> After Nicholas V died in early 1455, the new pope <a href="/wiki/Callixtus_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Callixtus III">Callixtus III</a> gave permission for a rehabilitation trial, and appointed three commissioners to oversee the process: <a href="/wiki/Jean_Juv%C3%A9nal_des_Ursins" title="Jean Juvénal des Ursins">Jean Juvénal des Ursins</a>, archbishop of Reims; <a href="/wiki/Guillaume_Chartier_(bishop)" title="Guillaume Chartier (bishop)">Guillaume Chartier</a>, bishop of Paris; and <a href="/wiki/Richard_Olivier_de_Longueil" title="Richard Olivier de Longueil">Richard Olivier de Longueil</a>, bishop of <a href="/wiki/Coutances" title="Coutances">Coutances</a>. They chose Bréhal as Inquisitor.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage124_124&#93;Lowell1896&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen369_351&#93;Murray1902&#91;httpswwwgutenbergorgfiles5738957389-h57389-hhtmPage_373_373&#93;_289-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage124_124]Lowell1896[httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen369_351]Murray1902[httpswwwgutenbergorgfiles5738957389-h57389-hhtmPage_373_373]-289">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The rehabilitation trial began on 7 November 1455 at Notre Dame Cathedral when Joan's mother publicly delivered a formal request for her daughter's rehabilitation,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage235_235&#93;Lowell1896&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen369_351&#93;Pernoud1955&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage37_37&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190&#93;_290-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage235_235]Lowell1896[httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen369_351]Pernoud1955[httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage37_37]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190]-290">&#91;284&#93;</a></sup> and ended on 7 July 1456 at <a href="/wiki/Rouen_Cathedral" title="Rouen Cathedral">Rouen Cathedral</a>, having heard from about 115 witnesses.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage156_156&#93;_291-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage156_156]-291">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup> The court found that the original trial was unjust and deceitful; Joan's abjuration, execution and their consequences were nullified.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage236_236&#93;Lowell1896&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen373_355&#93;Pernoud1955&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage287_287–288&#93;_292-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage236_236]Lowell1896[httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen373_355]Pernoud1955[httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage287_287–288]-292">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup> In his summary of the trial, Bréhal suggested that Cauchon and the assessors who supported him might be guilty of malice and heresy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENapier2017&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidH2AuDwAAQBApgPT62_67&#93;_293-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENapier2017[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidH2AuDwAAQBApgPT62_67]-293">&#91;287&#93;</a></sup> To emphasize the court's decision, a copy of the Articles of Accusation was formally torn up. The court ordered that a cross should be erected on the site of Joan's execution.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECastor2015&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page241_241&#93;Gies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage237_237&#93;Pernoud1962&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcbyherse00pernpage268_268&#93;_294-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECastor2015[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page241_241]Gies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage237_237]Pernoud1962[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcbyherse00pernpage268_268]-294">&#91;288&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Visions">Visions</span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Jeanne_d%27_Arc_(Eugene_Thirion).jpg" class="image"><img alt="Joan seated and looking forward with her furled banner while an angel whispers in her ear. An armored figure with fleur-de-lys banner is blowing a horn in the background." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Jeanne_d%27_Arc_%28Eugene_Thirion%29.jpg/170px-Jeanne_d%27_Arc_%28Eugene_Thirion%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="240" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Jeanne_d%27_Arc_%28Eugene_Thirion%29.jpg/255px-Jeanne_d%27_Arc_%28Eugene_Thirion%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Jeanne_d%27_Arc_%28Eugene_Thirion%29.jpg/340px-Jeanne_d%27_Arc_%28Eugene_Thirion%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="907" data-file-height="1280" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Jeanne_d%27_Arc_(Eugene_Thirion).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Jeanne d'Arc écoutant les voix</i> by Eugène Thirion (1876, Notre Dame Church, Ville de Chatou)</div></div></div>
<p>Joan's visions played an important role in her condemnation, and her admission that she had returned to heeding them led to her execution.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage217_217&#93;Hobbins2005&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailstrialofjoanofarc00danipage24_24–25&#93;Taylor2006&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage33_33&#93;_295-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage217_217]Hobbins2005[httpsarchiveorgdetailstrialofjoanofarc00danipage24_24–25]Taylor2006[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage33_33]-295">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup> Theologians of the era believed that visions could have a supernatural source.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage24_24&#93;Taylor2006&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage13_13&#93;,_&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage27_27&#93;_296-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage24_24]Taylor2006[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage13_13],_[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage27_27]-296">&#91;290&#93;</a></sup> The assessors at her trial focused on determining the specific source of Joan's visions,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage24_24&#93;Sullivan1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage86_86&#93;Weiskopf1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage127_127&#93;_297-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage24_24]Sullivan1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage86_86]Weiskopf1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage127_127]-297">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup> using an ecclesiastical form of <span title="Limburgish-language text"><i lang="li">discretio spirituum</i></span> (<a href="/wiki/Discernment_of_spirits" class="mw-redirect" title="Discernment of spirits">discernment of spirits</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESullivan1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage32_32&#93;_298-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESullivan1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage32_32]-298">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup> Because she was accused of heresy, they sought to show that her visions were false.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETaylor2006&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage29_29&#93;_299-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor2006[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage29_29]-299">&#91;293&#93;</a></sup> The rehabilitation trial nullified Joan's sentence, but did not declare her visions authentic.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage236_236&#93;Lightbody1961&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage140_140&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190&#93;_300-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage236_236]Lightbody1961[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage140_140]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage190_190]-300">&#91;294&#93;</a></sup> In 1894, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a> pronounced that Joan's mission was divinely inspired.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKelly1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage222_220–223&#93;_301-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKelly1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage222_220–223]-301">&#91;295&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Modern scholars have discussed possible neurological and psychiatric causes for her visions.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHarrison2014&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage35_35–36&#93;Henker1984&#91;httpspubmedncbinlmnihgov6390693_abstract&#93;Schildkrout2017&#91;httpsajppsychiatryonlineorgdoi101176appiajp201717080948_§6&#93;_302-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarrison2014[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage35_35–36]Henker1984[httpspubmedncbinlmnihgov6390693_abstract]Schildkrout2017[httpsajppsychiatryonlineorgdoi101176appiajp201717080948_§6]-302">&#91;296&#93;</a></sup> Her visions have been described as hallucinations arising from <a href="/wiki/Epilepsy" title="Epilepsy">epilepsy</a><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEd&#39;OrsiTinuper2006&#91;httpswwwepilepsybehaviorcomarticleS1525-50500600175-2fulltext_abstract&#93;Foote-SmithBayne1991&#91;httpsonlinelibrarywileycomdoi101111j1528-11571991tb05537x_abstract&#93;NicastroFabienne2016&#91;httpswwwepilepsybehaviorcomarticleS1525-50501500698-8fulltext_abstract&#93;_303-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEd&#39;OrsiTinuper2006[httpswwwepilepsybehaviorcomarticleS1525-50500600175-2fulltext_abstract]Foote-SmithBayne1991[httpsonlinelibrarywileycomdoi101111j1528-11571991tb05537x_abstract]NicastroFabienne2016[httpswwwepilepsybehaviorcomarticleS1525-50501500698-8fulltext_abstract]-303">&#91;297&#93;</a></sup> or a temporal lobe <a href="/wiki/Tuberculoma" title="Tuberculoma">tuberculoma</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERatnasuriya1986&#91;httpsjournalssagepubcomdoipdf101177014107688607900413_235&#93;_304-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERatnasuriya1986[httpsjournalssagepubcomdoipdf101177014107688607900413_235]-304">&#91;298&#93;</a></sup> Others have implicated <a href="/wiki/Ergot#Effects_on_humans,_other_mammals_and_LSD" title="Ergot">ergot poisoning</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShermanZimmerman2008&#91;httpswwwtandfonlinecomdoiabs101300J053v02n03_08_abstract&#93;_305-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShermanZimmerman2008[httpswwwtandfonlinecomdoiabs101300J053v02n03_08_abstract]-305">&#91;299&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Schizophrenia" title="Schizophrenia">schizophrenia</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAllen1975&#91;httpswebarchiveorgweb20131228212724httpswwwmedievalistsnetfiles09012321pdf_4–7&#93;_306-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllen1975[httpswebarchiveorgweb20131228212724httpswwwmedievalistsnetfiles09012321pdf_4–7]-306">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Delusional_disorder" title="Delusional disorder">delusional disorder</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMackowiak2007&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailspostmortemsolvin0000mackpage140_140&#93;_307-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMackowiak2007[httpsarchiveorgdetailspostmortemsolvin0000mackpage140_140]-307">&#91;301&#93;</a></sup> or creative <a href="/wiki/Psychopathy" title="Psychopathy">psychopathy</a> induced by her early childhood rearing.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHenderson1939_308-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHenderson1939-308">&#91;302&#93;</a></sup> One of the <a href="/wiki/Devil%27s_advocate" title="Devil&#39;s advocate">Promoters of the Faith</a> at her 1903 canonization trial argued that her visions may have been manifestations of <a href="/wiki/Hysteria" title="Hysteria">hysteria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKelly1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage220_220&#93;_309-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKelly1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage220_220]-309">&#91;303&#93;</a></sup> Other scholars argue that Joan created some of the visions' specific details in response to the demands of the interrogators at her trial.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHuizinga1959&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsmenideashistoryt00huizpage223_223–224&#93;Sullivan1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage104_104–105&#93;Taylor2009&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidsV9kFj3O1noCpgPT37_37&#93;–&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidsV9kFj3O1noCpgPT38_38&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage130_130–131&#93;_310-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuizinga1959[httpsarchiveorgdetailsmenideashistoryt00huizpage223_223–224]Sullivan1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage104_104–105]Taylor2009[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidsV9kFj3O1noCpgPT37_37]–[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidsV9kFj3O1noCpgPT38_38]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage130_130–131]-310">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Many of these explanations have been challenged;<sup id="cite_ref-312" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-312">&#91;g&#93;</a></sup> the trial records designed to demonstrate that Joan was guilty of heresy are unlikely to provide the objective descriptions of symptoms needed to support a medical diagnosis.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEde_Toffol201681_313-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEde_Toffol201681-313">&#91;306&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Joan's firm belief in the divinity of her visions strengthened her confidence, enabled her to trust herself,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDeVries1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsisbn_9780750918053page38_38–39&#93;Gies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage28_28&#93;Henderson1939Schildkrout2017&#91;httpsajppsychiatryonlineorgdoi101176appiajp201717080948_§8&#93;_314-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeVries1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsisbn_9780750918053page38_38–39]Gies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage28_28]Henderson1939Schildkrout2017[httpsajppsychiatryonlineorgdoi101176appiajp201717080948_§8]-314">&#91;307&#93;</a></sup> and gave her hope during her capture and trial.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESullivan1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage140_140&#93;_315-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESullivan1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage140_140]-315">&#91;308&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Clothing">Clothing</span></h2>
<p>Joan's <a href="/wiki/Cross-dressing" title="Cross-dressing">cross-dressing</a> was the topic of five of the articles of accusation against her during the trial.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGarber1993&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsvestedinterestsc00garbpage215_215&#93;Schibanoff1996_316-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGarber1993[httpsarchiveorgdetailsvestedinterestsc00garbpage215_215]Schibanoff1996-316">&#91;309&#93;</a></sup> In the view of the assessors, it was the emblem of her heresy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA66_66&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage117_117&#93;Schibanoff1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage31_31&#93;_317-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA66_66]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage117_117]Schibanoff1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage31_31]-317">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup> Her final condemnation began when she was found to have resumed wearing men's clothes,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage217_217–218&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage132_132&#93;Schibanoff1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage31_31&#93;Sullivan1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage132_132&#93;_318-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage217_217–218]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage132_132]Schibanoff1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage31_31]Sullivan1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage132_132]-318">&#91;311&#93;</a></sup> which was taken as a sign that she had relapsed into heresy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA66_66&#93;Schibanoff1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage38_38&#93;_319-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA66_66]Schibanoff1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage38_38]-319">&#91;312&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_Jeanne_Arc_place_Pyramides_Paris_4_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="see caption" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Statue_Jeanne_Arc_place_Pyramides_Paris_4_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Statue_Jeanne_Arc_place_Pyramides_Paris_4_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Statue_Jeanne_Arc_place_Pyramides_Paris_4_%28cropped%29.jpg/255px-Statue_Jeanne_Arc_place_Pyramides_Paris_4_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Statue_Jeanne_Arc_place_Pyramides_Paris_4_%28cropped%29.jpg/340px-Statue_Jeanne_Arc_place_Pyramides_Paris_4_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="5184" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_Jeanne_Arc_place_Pyramides_Paris_4_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Jeanne_d%27Arc_(Fr%C3%A9miet)" title="Jeanne d&#39;Arc (Frémiet)"><i>Jeanne d'Arc</i></a>, a gilded bronze statue by <a href="/wiki/Emmanuel_Fr%C3%A9miet" title="Emmanuel Frémiet">Emmanuel Frémiet</a> (1874, <a href="/wiki/Place_des_Pyramides" title="Place des Pyramides">Place des Pyramides</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>From the time of her journey to Chinon to her abjuration, Joan usually wore men's clothes<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECrane2002&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsperformanceofsel0000cranpage74_74&#93;Fraioli2000&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcearlyde0000fraipage28_28,_fn18&#93;_320-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrane2002[httpsarchiveorgdetailsperformanceofsel0000cranpage74_74]Fraioli2000[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcearlyde0000fraipage28_28,_fn18]-320">&#91;313&#93;</a></sup> and cropped her hair in a <a href="/wiki/Bowl_cut" title="Bowl cut">male fashion</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECrane1996&#91;httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_307&#93;Schibanoff1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage42_42&#93;_321-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrane1996[httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_307]Schibanoff1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage42_42]-321">&#91;314&#93;</a></sup> When she left Vaucouleurs to see the Dauphin in Chinon, Joan was said to have worn a black <a href="/wiki/Doublet_(clothing)" title="Doublet (clothing)">doublet</a>, a black tunic, and a short black cap.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECrane1996&#91;httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_307&#93;_322-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrane1996[httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_307]-322">&#91;315&#93;</a></sup> By the time she was captured, she had acquired more elaborate outfits. At her trial, she was accused of wearing <a href="/wiki/Breeches" title="Breeches">breeches</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Mantle_(clothing)" title="Mantle (clothing)">mantle</a>, a coat of <a href="/wiki/Chain_mail" title="Chain mail">mail</a>, a doublet, hose joined to the doublet with twenty laces, tight boots, spurs, a <a href="/wiki/Breastplate" title="Breastplate">breastplate</a>, <a href="/wiki/Buskins" class="mw-redirect" title="Buskins">buskins</a>, a sword, a dagger, and a lance. She was also described as wearing furs, a golden <a href="/wiki/Surcoat" title="Surcoat">surcoat</a> over her armor, and sumptuous <a href="/wiki/Riding_habit" title="Riding habit">riding habits</a> made of precious cloth.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage192_192&#93;Lucie-Smith1976&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage34_34&#93;_323-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage192_192]Lucie-Smith1976[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage34_34]-323">&#91;316&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the trial proceedings, Joan is not recorded as giving a practical reason why she cross-dressed.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA67_67&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage144_144&#93;_324-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA67_67]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage144_144]-324">&#91;317&#93;</a></sup> She stated that it was her own choice to wear men's clothes,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage35_35–37&#93;Sackville-West1936&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailssaintjoanofarcbo0000sackpage91_91–92&#93;_325-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage35_35–37]Sackville-West1936[httpsarchiveorgdetailssaintjoanofarcbo0000sackpage91_91–92]-325">&#91;318&#93;</a></sup> and that she did so not at the request of men but by the command of God and his angels.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECrane1996&#91;httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_298&#93;Garber1993&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsvestedinterestsc00garbpage216_216&#93;Lucie-Smith1976&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage32_32–33&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage144_144–146&#93;_326-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrane1996[httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_298]Garber1993[httpsarchiveorgdetailsvestedinterestsc00garbpage216_216]Lucie-Smith1976[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarc0000lucipage32_32–33]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage144_144–146]-326">&#91;319&#93;</a></sup> She stated she would return to wearing women's clothes when she fulfilled her calling.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESullivan2011&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable23347178_316&#93;_327-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESullivan2011[httpswwwjstororgstable23347178_316]-327">&#91;320&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although Joan's cross-dressing was used to justify her execution, the church's position on it was not clear. In general, it was seen as a sin, but there was no agreement about its severity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA61_61&#93;_328-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA61_61]-328">&#91;321&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Aquinas" title="Thomas Aquinas">Thomas Aquinas</a> stated that a woman may wear a man's clothes to hide herself from enemies or if no other clothes were available,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESullivan1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage43_42&#93;_329-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESullivan1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage43_42]-329">&#91;322&#93;</a></sup> and Joan did both, wearing them in enemy territory to get to Chinon,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESackville-West1936&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailssaintjoanofarcbo0000sackpage92_92–93&#93;Schibanoff1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage41_41&#93;_330-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESackville-West1936[httpsarchiveorgdetailssaintjoanofarcbo0000sackpage92_92–93]Schibanoff1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage41_41]-330">&#91;323&#93;</a></sup> and in her prison cell after her abjuration when her dress was taken from her.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA66_66&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage132_132&#93;_331-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA66_66]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage132_132]-331">&#91;324&#93;</a></sup> Soon after the siege of Orléans was lifted, Jean Gerson said that Joan's male clothes and haircut were appropriate for her calling, as she was a warrior and men's clothes were more practical.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECrane1996&#91;httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_301&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage184_184&#93;_332-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrane1996[httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_301]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage184_184]-332">&#91;325&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cross-dressing may have helped her maintain her virginity by deterring rape<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDworkin1987&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsintercourse0000dworpage125_125–126&#93;Gies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage216_216&#93;Harrison2014&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage251_251–252&#93;Hotchkiss2000&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA67_67&#93;_333-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDworkin1987[httpsarchiveorgdetailsintercourse0000dworpage125_125–126]Gies1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage216_216]Harrison2014[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage251_251–252]Hotchkiss2000[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA67_67]-333">&#91;326&#93;</a></sup> and signaling her unavailability as a sexual object;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDworkin1987&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsintercourse0000dworpage126_126&#93;Schibanoff1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage52_52&#93;_334-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDworkin1987[httpsarchiveorgdetailsintercourse0000dworpage126_126]Schibanoff1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage52_52]-334">&#91;327&#93;</a></sup> scholars have stated that when she was imprisoned, wearing men's clothes would have only been a minor deterrent to rape as she was shackled most of the time.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA64_64–65&#93;Schibanoff1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage58_58&#93;_335-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHotchkiss2000[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidfrPPPIJvUDgCpgPA64_64–65]Schibanoff1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage58_58]-335">&#91;328&#93;</a></sup> For most of her active life, Joan did not cross-dress to hide her gender.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBullough1974Crane1996&#91;httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_310&#93;Sproles1996&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable23539706_163&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage147_147&#93;_336-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBullough1974Crane1996[httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_310]Sproles1996[httpswwwjstororgstable23539706_163]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage147_147]-336">&#91;329&#93;</a></sup> Rather, it may have functioned to emphasize her unique identity<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECrane2002&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsperformanceofsel0000cranpage78_78&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage142_142&#93;_337-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrane2002[httpsarchiveorgdetailsperformanceofsel0000cranpage78_78]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage142_142]-337">&#91;330&#93;</a></sup> as <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">La Pucelle</i></span>, a model of virtue that transcends <a href="/wiki/Gender_roles" class="mw-redirect" title="Gender roles">gender roles</a> and inspires people.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECrane1996&#91;httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_305–306&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage146_146–147&#93;_338-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrane1996[httpswebarchiveorgweb20160705094811httpsfacultysmuedubwheelerjoan_of_arcolr08_clothingjoanofarc_cranepdf_305–306]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage146_146–147]-338">&#91;331&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span></h2>
<p>Joan is one of the most studied people of the <a href="/wiki/Middle_Ages" title="Middle Ages">Middle Ages</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDeVries1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepagen19_3&#93;_339-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeVries1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepagen19_3]-339">&#91;332&#93;</a></sup> partly because her two trials provided a wealth of documents.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELightbody1961&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage16_16–17&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage6_4–6&#93;_340-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELightbody1961[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage16_16–17]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage6_4–6]-340">&#91;333&#93;</a></sup> Her image, changing over time, has included being the savior of France, an obedient daughter of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church">Roman Catholic Church</a>, an early feminist, and a symbol of freedom and independence.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESexsmith1990&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable42630458_125&#93;,_&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable42630458seq5_129&#93;_341-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESexsmith1990[httpswwwjstororgstable42630458_125],_[httpswwwjstororgstable42630458seq5_129]-341">&#91;334&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military_leader_and_symbol_of_France">Military leader and symbol of France</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_of_Jeanne_d%27Arc_in_Orl%C3%A9ans_A_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="Joan of Arc on horseback, with sword in right hand" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Statue_of_Jeanne_d%27Arc_in_Orl%C3%A9ans_A_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Statue_of_Jeanne_d%27Arc_in_Orl%C3%A9ans_A_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="198" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Statue_of_Jeanne_d%27Arc_in_Orl%C3%A9ans_A_%28cropped%29.jpg/255px-Statue_of_Jeanne_d%27Arc_in_Orl%C3%A9ans_A_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Statue_of_Jeanne_d%27Arc_in_Orl%C3%A9ans_A_%28cropped%29.jpg/340px-Statue_of_Jeanne_d%27Arc_in_Orl%C3%A9ans_A_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3702" data-file-height="4303" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_of_Jeanne_d%27Arc_in_Orl%C3%A9ans_A_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Joan of Arc</i>, statue by <a href="/wiki/Denis_Foyatier" title="Denis Foyatier">Denis Foyatier</a> (1855, <a href="/wiki/Orl%C3%A9ans" title="Orléans">Orléans</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>Joan's reputation as a military leader who helped drive the English from France began to form before her death. Just after Charles's coronation, <a href="/wiki/Christine_de_Pizan" title="Christine de Pizan">Christine de Pizan</a> wrote the poem <i>Ditié de Jehanne D'Arc</i>, celebrating Joan as a supporter of Charles sent by <a href="/wiki/Divine_Providence" class="mw-redirect" title="Divine Providence">Divine Providence</a>; the poem captured the "surge of optimism" and "sense of wonder and gratitude" that "swept through the whole of the French" after the triumph at Orléans, according to Kennedy and Varty (1977).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKennedyVarty1977&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidqogHAQAAIAAJpgPA1_1&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage25_25&#93;_342-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKennedyVarty1977[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidqogHAQAAIAAJpgPA1_1]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage25_25]-342">&#91;335&#93;</a></sup> As early as 1429, Orléans began holding a celebration in honor of the raising of the siege on 8 May.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHamblin2003&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombookside04BDgAAQBAJpgPA209_209&#93;_343-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHamblin2003[httpsbooksgooglecombookside04BDgAAQBAJpgPA209_209]-343">&#91;336&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After Joan's execution, her role in the Orléans victory encouraged popular support for her rehabilitation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELightbody1961&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage118_118&#93;_344-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELightbody1961[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage118_118]-344">&#91;337&#93;</a></sup> Joan became a central part of the annual celebration, and by 1435 a play, <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Mistère du siège d'Orléans</i></span> (Mystery of the Siege of Orléans),<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHamblin2003&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombookside04BDgAAQBAJpgPA217_217&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage243_243&#93;_345-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHamblin2003[httpsbooksgooglecombookside04BDgAAQBAJpgPA217_217]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage243_243]-345">&#91;338&#93;</a></sup> portrayed her as the vehicle of the divine will that liberated Orléans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHamblin1988&#91;httpsjstororgstable1347436seq3_63–64&#93;_346-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHamblin1988[httpsjstororgstable1347436seq3_63–64]-346">&#91;339&#93;</a></sup> The Orléans festival celebrating Joan continues in modern times.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOrléans2021PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage242_242–245&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage192_192&#93;_347-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOrléans2021PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage242_242–245]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage192_192]-347">&#91;340&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Less than a decade after her rehabilitation trial, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_II" title="Pope Pius II">Pope Pius II</a> wrote a brief biography describing her as the maid who saved the kingdom of France.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETaylor2006&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage350_350–352&#93;_348-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor2006[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage350_350–352]-348">&#91;341&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Louis_XII" title="Louis XII">Louis XII</a> commissioned a full-length biography of her around 1500.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHarrison2014&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage316_316&#93;RankinQuintal1964&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfirstbiographyof0000rankpage3_3&#93;_349-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarrison2014[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclifetra0000harrpage316_316]RankinQuintal1964[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfirstbiographyof0000rankpage3_3]-349">&#91;342&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Joan's early legacy was closely associated with the <a href="/wiki/Divine_right_of_kings" title="Divine right of kings">divine right</a> of the monarchy to rule France.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFraioli2000&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcearlyde0000fraipage56_56&#93;Mackinnon1902&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsgrowthdeclineoff00mackuoftpage78_78&#93;Wood1988&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcrichard0000woodpage150_150&#93;_350-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFraioli2000[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcearlyde0000fraipage56_56]Mackinnon1902[httpsarchiveorgdetailsgrowthdeclineoff00mackuoftpage78_78]Wood1988[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcrichard0000woodpage150_150]-350">&#91;343&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolution" title="French Revolution">French Revolution</a>, her reputation came into question because of her association with the monarchy and religion,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELightbody1961&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage15_15&#93;Mock2011&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage39_39&#93;_351-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELightbody1961[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjudgementsofjoan0000lighpage15_15]Mock2011[httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage39_39]-351">&#91;344&#93;</a></sup> and the festival in her honor held at Orléans was suspended in 1793.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrance1909&#91;httpswwwgutenbergorgfiles1948819488-h19488-hhtmINTRODUCTION_lix–lx&#93;_352-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrance1909[httpswwwgutenbergorgfiles1948819488-h19488-hhtmINTRODUCTION_lix–lx]-352">&#91;345&#93;</a></sup> In 1803, <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_Bonaparte" class="mw-redirect" title="Napoleon Bonaparte">Napoleon Bonaparte</a> authorized its renewal<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWarner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage256_256&#93;_353-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWarner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage256_256]-353">&#91;346&#93;</a></sup> and the creation of a new statue of Joan at Orléans, stating: "The illustrious Joan&#160;... proved that there is no miracle which French genius cannot accomplish when national independence is threatened."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEConner2004&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsageofnapoleon0000connpage89_89&#93;Guillemin1970&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage249_249&#93;_354-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEConner2004[httpsarchiveorgdetailsageofnapoleon0000connpage89_89]Guillemin1970[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage249_249]-354">&#91;347&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since then, she has become a prominent symbol as the defender of the French nation. After the French defeat in the <a href="/wiki/Franco-Prussian_War" title="Franco-Prussian War">Franco-Prussian War</a>, Joan became a rallying point for a new crusade to reclaim Lorraine, the province of her birth.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuillemin1970&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage250_250&#93;Maddox2012&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidrETxD8KcnUICpgPA444_444&#93;_355-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuillemin1970[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage250_250]Maddox2012[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidrETxD8KcnUICpgPA444_444]-355">&#91;348&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/French_Third_Republic" title="French Third Republic">Third Republic</a> held a patriotic civic holiday in her honor,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrown2012&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable43489248seq12_450&#93;Mock2011&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage144_144&#93;_356-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown2012[httpswwwjstororgstable43489248seq12_450]Mock2011[httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage144_144]-356">&#91;349&#93;</a></sup> on 8 May to celebrate her victory at Orléans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuillemin1970&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage254_255&#93;Sexsmith1990&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable42630458seq5_129&#93;_357-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuillemin1970[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage254_255]Sexsmith1990[httpswwwjstororgstable42630458seq5_129]-357">&#91;350&#93;</a></sup> During World War I, her image was used to inspire victory.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrown2012&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable43489248seq11_449&#93;Gaehtgens2018&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidYgldDwAAQBAJpgPA45_45&#93;_358-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown2012[httpswwwjstororgstable43489248seq11_449]Gaehtgens2018[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidYgldDwAAQBAJpgPA45_45]-358">&#91;351&#93;</a></sup> In World War II, all sides of the French cause appealed to her legacy:<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECohen2014&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDcfbAgAAQBAJpgPA130_130&#93;_359-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen2014[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDcfbAgAAQBAJpgPA130_130]-359">&#91;352&#93;</a></sup> she was a symbol for <a href="/wiki/Philippe_P%C3%A9tain" title="Philippe Pétain">Philippe Pétain</a> in <a href="/wiki/Vichy_France" title="Vichy France">Vichy France</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrown2012&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable43489248seq14_452&#93;Cohen2014&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDcfbAgAAQBAJpgPA130_130&#93;_360-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown2012[httpswwwjstororgstable43489248seq14_452]Cohen2014[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDcfbAgAAQBAJpgPA130_130]-360">&#91;353&#93;</a></sup> a model for <a href="/wiki/Charles_de_Gaulle" title="Charles de Gaulle">Charles de Gaulle</a>'s leadership of the <a href="/wiki/Free_French" class="mw-redirect" title="Free French">Free French</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECohen2014&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDcfbAgAAQBAJpgPA138_138&#93;Dunn2021&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidSG4HEAAAQBAJpgPA62_62&#93;_361-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen2014[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidDcfbAgAAQBAJpgPA138_138]Dunn2021[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidSG4HEAAAQBAJpgPA62_62]-361">&#91;354&#93;</a></sup> and an example for the <a href="/wiki/French_Resistance#Communists" title="French Resistance">Communist resistance</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMock2011&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage220_220&#93;_362-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMock2011[httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage220_220]-362">&#91;355&#93;</a></sup> More recently, her association with the monarchy and national liberation has made her a symbol for the French far right, including the monarchist movement <a href="/wiki/Action_Fran%C3%A7aise" title="Action Française">Action Française</a><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDunn2021&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidSG4HEAAAQBAJpgPA62_62&#93;_363-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDunn2021[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidSG4HEAAAQBAJpgPA62_62]-363">&#91;356&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/National_Rally" title="National Rally">National Front Party</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGildea1996&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksid-JcLOEKvJ2kCpgPA165_165&#93;Margolis1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage265_265&#93;_364-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGildea1996[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid-JcLOEKvJ2kCpgPA165_165]Margolis1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage265_265]-364">&#91;357&#93;</a></sup> Joan's image has been used by the entire spectrum of French politics,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrown2012&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable43489248_439&#93;Mock2011&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage3_3&#93;_365-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown2012[httpswwwjstororgstable43489248_439]Mock2011[httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage3_3]-365">&#91;358&#93;</a></sup> and she is an important reference in political dialogue about French identity and unity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMock2011&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage145_145&#93;_366-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMock2011[httpsarchiveorgdetailssymbolsofdefeati0000mockpage145_145]-366">&#91;359&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Saint_and_heroic_woman">Saint and heroic woman</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Albert_Lynch_-_Jeanne_d%27Arc.jpg" class="image"><img alt="See caption" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Albert_Lynch_-_Jeanne_d%27Arc.jpg/170px-Albert_Lynch_-_Jeanne_d%27Arc.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="234" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Albert_Lynch_-_Jeanne_d%27Arc.jpg/255px-Albert_Lynch_-_Jeanne_d%27Arc.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Albert_Lynch_-_Jeanne_d%27Arc.jpg/340px-Albert_Lynch_-_Jeanne_d%27Arc.jpg 2x" data-file-width="770" data-file-height="1062" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Albert_Lynch_-_Jeanne_d%27Arc.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Illustration by <a href="/wiki/Albert_Lynch" title="Albert Lynch">Albert Lynch</a> (1903, in <i>Figaro Illustré</i> magazine)</div></div></div>
<p>Joan is a <a href="/wiki/Saint#Catholic_church" title="Saint">saint</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church">Roman Catholic Church</a>. She was viewed as a religious figure in Orléans after the siege was lifted and an annual <a href="/wiki/Panegyric" title="Panegyric">panegyric</a> was pronounced there on her behalf until the 1800s.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGildea1996&#91;httpswwwgooglecombookseditionThe_Past_in_French_History-JcLOEKvJ2kChlengbpv1bsqpanegyric_155–156&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage311_311–312,_fn_24&#93;_367-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGildea1996[httpswwwgooglecombookseditionThe_Past_in_French_History-JcLOEKvJ2kChlengbpv1bsqpanegyric_155–156]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage311_311–312,_fn_24]-367">&#91;360&#93;</a></sup> In 1849, the Bishop of Orlėans <a href="/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Dupanloup" title="Félix Dupanloup">Félix Dupanloup</a> delivered an oration that attracted international attention,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETaylor2012&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable23240136seq223_238&#93;_368-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor2012[httpswwwjstororgstable23240136seq223_238]-368">&#91;361&#93;</a></sup> and in 1869 petitioned Rome to begin <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a> proceedings.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGildea1996PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage244_244–245&#93;Taylor2012&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable23240136seq23_238&#93;_369-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGildea1996PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage244_244–245]Taylor2012[httpswwwjstororgstable23240136seq23_238]-369">&#91;362&#93;</a></sup> She was beatified by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a> in 1909, and <a href="/wiki/Canonization_of_Joan_of_Arc" title="Canonization of Joan of Arc">canonized</a> on 16 May 1920 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XV" title="Pope Benedict XV">Pope Benedict XV</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage245_245&#93;Taylor2012&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable23240136seq25_240&#93;_370-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage245_245]Taylor2012[httpswwwjstororgstable23240136seq25_240]-370">&#91;363&#93;</a></sup> Her <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">feast day</a> is 30 May, the anniversary of her execution.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECastor2015&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page244_244&#93;_371-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECastor2015[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page244_244]-371">&#91;364&#93;</a></sup> In an <a href="/wiki/Ecclesiastical_letter" title="Ecclesiastical letter">apostolic letter</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a> declared Joan one of the patron saints of France on 2 March 1922.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPius_XI1922&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailssim_acta-apostolicae-sedis_1922-04-03_14_7page187_187&#93;_372-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPius_XI1922[httpsarchiveorgdetailssim_acta-apostolicae-sedis_1922-04-03_14_7page187_187]-372">&#91;365&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Joan was canonized as a <a href="/wiki/Virgin_(title)" title="Virgin (title)">Virgin</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESullivan1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage162_162&#93;_373-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESullivan1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage162_162]-373">&#91;366&#93;</a></sup> not as a <a href="/wiki/Christian_martyr" title="Christian martyr">Christian martyr</a><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChenu1990&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsbookofchristianm00chenpage98_98&#93;Ghezzi1996Sullivan1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage106_106_fn8&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage264_264&#93;_374-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChenu1990[httpsarchiveorgdetailsbookofchristianm00chenpage98_98]Ghezzi1996Sullivan1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage106_106_fn8]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage264_264]-374">&#91;367&#93;</a></sup> because she had been put to death by a canonically constituted court,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuillemin1970&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage256_256&#93;_375-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuillemin1970[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage256_256]-375">&#91;368&#93;</a></sup> which did not execute for her faith in Christ,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHarrison2002&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidF3qFtB-Lq14CpgPA105_105&#93;_376-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarrison2002[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidF3qFtB-Lq14CpgPA105_105]-376">&#91;369&#93;</a></sup> but for her private revelation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKelly1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage210_210&#93;_377-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKelly1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage210_210]-377">&#91;370&#93;</a></sup> Nevertheless, she has been popularly venerated as a martyr since her death:<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELowell1896&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen360_842&#93;Meltzer2001&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_SEPFzzpvp0CpgPA192_192&#93;Pernoud1955&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage6_6&#93;,_&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage252_252&#93;Taylor2006&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage29_29_fn86&#93;_378-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELowell1896[httpsarchiveorgdetailsJoanOfArc1896pagen360_842]Meltzer2001[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_SEPFzzpvp0CpgPA192_192]Pernoud1955[httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage6_6],_[httpsarchiveorgdetailsretrialofjoanofa00regipage252_252]Taylor2006[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclapucel00unsepage29_29_fn86]-378">&#91;371&#93;</a></sup> one who suffered for her modesty and purity,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKelly1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage210_210&#93;Michelet1855&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcmaidofo00michpage249_249&#93;McInerney2003&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidgrHIDAAAQBAJpgPA210_210&#93;–&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidgrHIDAAAQBAJpgPA211_211&#93;Sullivan1999&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage30_30–31&#93;_379-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKelly1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage210_210]Michelet1855[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcmaidofo00michpage249_249]McInerney2003[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidgrHIDAAAQBAJpgPA210_210]–[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidgrHIDAAAQBAJpgPA211_211]Sullivan1999[httpsarchiveorgdetailsinterrogationofj00sullpage30_30–31]-379">&#91;372&#93;</a></sup>
her country,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKelly1996&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage210_210&#93;Guillemin1970&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage249_249&#93;Warner1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage268_268&#93;_380-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKelly1996[httpsarchiveorgdetailsfreshverdictsonj0000unsepage210_210]Guillemin1970[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanmaidoforlean0000guilpage249_249]Warner1981[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcimageof0000warnpage268_268]-380">&#91;373&#93;</a></sup>
and the strength of her convictions.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChenu1990&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsbookofchristianm00chenpage98_98–99&#93;_381-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChenu1990[httpsarchiveorgdetailsbookofchristianm00chenpage98_98–99]-381">&#91;374&#93;</a></sup> Joan is also <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">remembered as a visionary</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Commemoration_(observance)" class="mw-redirect" title="Commemoration (observance)">commemoration</a> on 30 May.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThe_Calendar2021_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThe_Calendar2021-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> She is revered in the <a href="/wiki/Pantheon_(religion)" title="Pantheon (religion)">pantheon</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Cao_Dai" class="mw-redirect" title="Cao Dai">Cao Dai</a> religion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBoal2005&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsintroductiontowo2005unsepage208_208&#93;_382-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoal2005[httpsarchiveorgdetailsintroductiontowo2005unsepage208_208]-382">&#91;375&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While Joan was alive, she was already being compared to biblical women heroes, such as <a href="/wiki/Esther" title="Esther">Esther</a>, <a href="/wiki/Judith#Main_characters" class="mw-redirect" title="Judith">Judith</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Deborah" title="Deborah">Deborah</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFraioli1981&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable2847364_811&#93;,_&#91;httpswwwjstororgstable2847364seq3_813–814&#93;_383-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFraioli1981[httpswwwjstororgstable2847364_811],_[httpswwwjstororgstable2847364seq3_813–814]-383">&#91;376&#93;</a></sup> Her claim of virginity, which signified her virtue and sincerity,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDworkin1987&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsintercourse0000dworpage126_126–127&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage30_30–31&#93;Meltzer2001&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_SEPFzzpvp0CpgPA94_94&#93;_384-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDworkin1987[httpsarchiveorgdetailsintercourse0000dworpage126_126–127]PernoudClin1986[httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage30_30–31]Meltzer2001[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_SEPFzzpvp0CpgPA94_94]-384">&#91;377&#93;</a></sup> was upheld by women of status from both the Armagnac and Burgundian-English sides of the Hundred Years' War: Yolande of Aragon, Charles's mother-in-law, and <a href="/wiki/Anne_of_Burgundy" title="Anne of Burgundy">Anne of Burgundy</a>, Duchess of Bedford.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECastor2015&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page97_97&#93;,_&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarchistory0000cast_n6r7page168_168&#93;Gies1981&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage54_54&#93;,_&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarclegendr0000giespage154_154&#93;PernoudClin1986&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage30_30–31&#93;,_&#91;httpsarchiveorgdetailsjoanofarcherstor00pernpage105_105&#93;_385-0" class="reference"><a
